The                process includes condensing various hydrocarbon                fractions in different receptacles and condensers,                such as C7-C10 and C10-C14 hydrocarbons. The C14                and lighter hydrocarbon fractions are introduced                into a hot condenser, C7 and lighter hydrocarbons                into a cool condenser, and C5 and lighter                hydrocarbons into a gas distributor and a gas                bubbler of a dual fuel diesel generator. The                heavier hydrocarbon fractions are condensed in                different receptacles and condensers based on their                molecular weight. The process involves multiple                steps of introducing, separating, condensing, and                repeating the process for different hydrocarbon                fractions until all the desired products are                obtained.    USE - Catalyst-free process for upcycling plastics                to chemicals.    ADVANTAGE - The process aims to efficiently convert                plastic waste into valuable chemicals without need                for catalyst, highlighting a sustainable approach                towards plastic waste management and chemical                production. USE - The compound is useful: in upcycling plastic                (claimed) into value-added chemicals, preferably                into useful products for other industrial uses i.e.                formic acid as liquid organic hydrogen carrier,                which serves as a type of clean fuel, benzoic acid                as food preservatives, acetic acid as food                additive, solvents and antiseptics, acetophenone as                fragrance, and terephthalic acid and ethylene                glycol as monomer of PET synthesis; for developing                metal-free photocatalysts for converting a diverse                range of plastics and mixed plastics into                high-value compounds at ambient conditions; and for                upcycling co-polymers, mixed plastics,                post-consumer plastic wastes, and contaminated                real-life plastic waste at ambient conditions                including room temperature and atmospheric                pressure.    ADVANTAGE - The compound: has a specific surface area of                90-180 m2/g, preferably 120.35 m2/g, has many pores                having a diameter of 1-120 nm, preferably 5-100 nm,                has an optical band gap of 2-3.5 eV, preferably 2.4                eV, has a conduction band minimum (CBM) of -1 to                -1.25 V versus normal hydrogen electrode (NHE),                preferably -1.18 V versus NHE, has a valence band                maximum (VBM) of 1-1.7 V versus NHE, preferably                1.22 V versus NHE, and has an Urbach energy of                0.1-0.25 eV, preferably 0.19 eV; is capable of at                least partially converting a plastic into smaller                molecules upon irradiation with light; is                metal-free, hence does not lead to any secondary                pollution which originates from the metal; performs                entire conversion process at ambient conditions                including room temperature and atmospheric                pressure, with visible light as the only source of                energy input to drive the conversion, in contrast                to the literature work that require elevated                temperatures for reactions to proceed; and requires                raw materials, preferably urea and ammonium                acetate, for the synthesis of the photocatalyst                that are cheap, abundant, and readily available,                while involving simple calcination and providing                easily scaled up production.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are also included                for:    ynthesizing (M1) the compound; and    upcycling (M2) plastics, comprising (i)                providing a plastic, a solvent system, and the                compound to form a reaction mixture, and (ii)                irradiating the reaction mixture with a light                source for a third period of time, so that the                plastic is at least partially converted into                smaller molecules. USE - Large-scale ultrafast synthesis of nickel-iron                oxide catalyst used in upcycling of PET plastic                (all claimed).    ADVANTAGE - The method is simple and suitable for                large-scale production, and not only reduces the                potential of the anodic reaction, but also converts                waste plastics into high-value-added chemicals for                use as industrial raw materials.  AU2021253856-A1;  CN115551626-A;  CA3174749-A1;  EP4132696-A1;  US2023167367-A1;  HK40082093-A0;  EP4132696-A4;  US12312539-B2;  US2025282997-A1	Reactor vessel for liquid phase processing of            plastics, has cylindrical reactor barrel, and arm set            having distal end to support shear plate extending            parallel to central shaft at spacing to generate            predetermined strain rate	MCCLURE V E	MCCLURE V E (MCCL-Individual);  MCCLURE V E (MCCL-Individual);  MCCLURE V E (MCCL-Individual);  MCCLURE V E (MCCL-Individual)	2021B6584D	   NOVELTY - Reactor vessel (104) for liquid phase                processing of plastics, comprises a cylindrical                reactor barrel having a barrel diameter and a                barrel length; a heat exchange shell (136); a                reactor top plate disposed at a top of the reactor                barrel; a reactor bottom plate disposed at a bottom                of the reactor barrel; a central shaft (146)                supported concentrically and rotatably within the                reactor barrel; at least one arm set extending                radially from the central shaft, each arm having a                distal end configured to support a shear plate                extending parallel to the central shaft at a                spacing from an inner wall of the reactor barrel,                the spacing configured to generate a predetermined                strain rate between the shear plate and the inner                wall via Couette flow; a drive motor (112)                supported by the reactor top plate and configured                for driving rotation of the central shaft; an inlet                for introducing fluid into the reactor barrel for                treatment; and at least one outlet.    USE - The reactor vessel is useful for liquid phase                processing (recycling or upcycling) of plastics;                and useful in processing facility (all                claimed).    ADVANTAGE - The shear plates are spaced from the vessel                wall to generate a predetermined strain rate                between the shear plate and the inner wall via                Couette flow, thus enhancing efficiency of the                reaction. The process provides an improved method                of supplying heat to the reaction vessels. It also                improves the manner by which heat is transmitted                into the reactants, increases the efficiency of                mixing of the reactants and catalyst and                facilitates handling of elemental carbon by-product                and un-reactive solids in the feed stock. The new                shear plate reactor design provides efficiency and                effectiveness of a multi-reactor facility, and                makes possible the creation of catalytic                de-polymerization (CDP) plants with capacities of                100 tons/day and higher. Compared to a single large                reaction vessel, multiple reaction vessels                connected in series, while not a true continuous,                i.e. non-stop, process, can easily approach the                effect of a continuous process by controlling the                residence time of reactants within the different                reactors. When heating controls the rate, the                smaller tanks in series have an even greater                advantage over a single tank being used in a                (pseudo-) continuous process since the smaller                tanks have more area of thinner walls in contact                per unit volume of process fluid. Moisture and                volatile components of the feed material are                removed at low temperature, thus saving the energy                that would have been used to increase their                temperature to that of a single hot reactor.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for:    processing facility comprising reactor                vessels, where the reactor vessels are connected in                series; and    recycling or upcycling of plastics which                involves mixing a dispersion of particles of                plastic, solvent oil and a catalyst to form a                slurry; heating the slurry to a processing                temperature; feeding the heated slurry into the                processing facility or reactor vessel to process                the slurry. USE - The process is used for manufacturing a                thermoformable and/or embossable paper-based                particle/polymer composite, such as a molded                article. The molded article is used as an element                in structures such as wall panels, room dividers,                floors, counters or in furniture (all claimed). The                process is used for the manufacturing of                particle/polymer moldings (molded articles), such                as elements in buildings such as wall panels, room                dividers, floors, tiles, counters and in furniture,                and components for car interiors, music instruments                or any other suitable application. The process is                used for the manufacturing of products like molded                articles for a variety of possible applications                (car fittings, molded car interior components, and                components of music instruments) or elements used                in buildings, such as wall panels, room dividers,                floors, tiles, counters and in furniture, and                generally any components or elements which are                suitable to replace conventional components or                elements made of plastic or wood (veneers,                wood-based boards like particle boards, medium                density fiberboard (MDF), oriented strand board                (OSB) and others which are usually prepared using                formaldehyde containing resins).    ADVANTAGE - The process handles the arising amounts of                polymer-coated paper product wastes and is a                cost-effective for upcycling polymer-coated paper                products. The composite can be produced at low                cost. The processs provides a cost and energy                saving process for recycling/upcycling paper waste                products. The process allows the recycling, and an                upcycling to new products of high quality different                from typical paper products. The process produces                molded articles in a much more time and energy                saving way and with more cost-effective and more                environmentally-friendly and sustainable.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - Process of manufacturing a thermoformable                and/or embossable paper-based particle/polymer                composite using a particulate substrate S                comprising shredded polymer-glued or polymer-coated                paper products and at least one polymer P,                comprises: (i) introducing the particulate                substrate S into a gas stream, (ii) contacting the                particulate substrate S with an aqueous dispersion                or solution of the polymer P having a glass                transition temperature (TgP) in the gas stream,                (iii) drying the particulate substrate S contacted                with the polymer P in the gas stream and                depositing, (iv) transferring the obtained                deposited polymer-contacted substrate into a layer,                a floor or into a mold of a desired shape, and (v)                densifying the polymer-contacted substrate at a                temperature greater than or equal to the glass                transition temperature Tg of the polymer P to form                the thermoformable and/or embossable paper-based                particle/polymer composite, where the polymer P is                thermoplastic and has TgP of &#8805;                -20&#176; C measured according to DIN EN ISO                11357-2 (2013-09). The shredded recycled plastic is                washed and dried (300). The biomass and crushed                recycled plastic are mixed (400) to form a base                material. The pretreatment mixture is manufactured                (500) by melting, extruding and foam-molding base                material to form a foam layer. An insulation sheet                is formed (600) by coating first outer layer on the                upper side of the pretreatment mixture and coating                second outer layer on the lower side, or the                insulation sheet is formed by coating the first                outer layer on the upper side of the foam layer and                coating second outer layer on the lower side.    USE - Method for manufacturing insulation sheet such                as foamed building insulation sheet by mixing                discarded bottle caps with biomass. Uses include                but are not limited to building insulation                materials, home appliance cushioning packaging                materials, agricultural and marine product boxes,                aquaculture floats, food and pharmaceutical                packaging containers, courier packaging materials,                helmet and surfboard interior materials, and other                industrial packaging materials.    ADVANTAGE - The biomass is mixed into a plastic bottle cap                during the upcycling process, so the polyethylene                content is relatively low, so that the sheet usable                as a building insulation material can be produced.                The advantage of solving problems of product                deformation or failure to secure foam uniformity by                forming a coating layer on the upper and lower                surfaces of the foam layer to prevent gas from                being emitted from the foam layer to the outside is                possible. The                method has capability to remove PP contamination in                PET recycled streams from existing plastic                recycling facility. The recovered PP may have                residual oil (less than 2 wt.%) attached to                surfaces or void spaces of the polymer. The method                can utilize waste resources and green solvents to                separately recover and upcycle PP and PE from mixed                plastic waste streams, and produce virgin-like high                quality recycled PP and PE from zero-value                feedstocks, and produce recycled PET without PP                contamination. The axle extends through                the first and second segments. The first segment                comprises a first helical blade (HB). A second                helical blade (HB') is positioned around a portion                of the peripheral surface of the axle and adapted                to flight a portion of the mixed waste stream in a                second opposite direction between the inlet end and                the outlet end of the axle. The first segment is                configured to convey portions of the mixed waste                stream in opposite directions by rotating the axle.                The second segment comprises a third helical blade                (HB'') adapted to flight a portion of the mixed                waste stream in the first or second direction. A                cylindrical disc (CD) comprises a wall section                extending in a radial direction, and is positioned                on the periphery of the axle between the first and                second segments.    USE - Two-way screw conveyor for a processing                chamber of a pyrolysis system for upcycling a mixed                waste stream into an upcycled material or product,                such as an oil or gas product, preferably such as a                fuel oil or fuel gas (all claimed).    ADVANTAGE - The uniform heating of the mixed waste stream                is ensured. The homogeneity of the mixed waste                stream is increased with the processing chamber of                reduced dimensions. An increased degassing of                volatile gasses from the processing chamber is                ensured, by increasing the mixing and heating of                the mixed waste stream. Blending of mixed-waste and                activating supplemental pH regulating additives are                ensured to ensure optimal conditions for pyrolysis                and the upcycling the mixed waste, such as                upcycling at least a portion of plastic waste into                an oil product.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - The first helical blade is positioned around a                portion of the peripheral surface of the axle and                adapted to flight a portion of the mixed waste                stream in a first direction between the inlet end                and the outlet end of the axle. The cylindrical                disc further comprises a cut-out section (CO) in                the wall section. The cut-out section enables for a                portion of the mixed waste stream to pass through                the cut-out section upon rotation of the axle. A                ratio between an area of the wall section of the                cylindrical disc and an area of the cutout section                in the wall section is defined, where the ratio is                between 20-1, preferably between 15-2, more                preferably between 10-3, even more preferably                between 8-4 and most preferably between 6-4.5. A                volume of mixed waste stream moved in a direction                from the inlet end to the outlet end, by the third                helical blade, per RPM of the axle, is higher than                a volume of mixed waste stream moved in the                direction from the inlet end to the outlet end, by                the first and second helical blades, revolutions                per minute (RPM) of the axle. The first and second                helical blades extend along the peripheral surface                of the axle, and intersect as a double helix with                opposite pitches of flight. The geometry of the                first and second helical blade is configured to mix                and convey the mixed waste stream in a first and                second direction, based on at least one of the                solid mass, liquid mass, texture, viscosity, and                porosity of the mixed waste stream. The first                segment is at the inlet end of the axle and the                second segment is at the outlet end of the axle.                The first and second helical blades are adapted to                flight the mixed waste stream towards the outlet                end at a first flight velocity and the third                helical blade is adapted to flight the mixed waste                stream at a second flight velocity. The second                flight velocity is greater than the first flight                velocity. Several sensors are adapted to measure                strain, temperature, force and RPM of the blades or                axle. INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for the                following:    a processing chamber adapted to be a section,                in a multi-section pyrolysis system for upcycling                mixed waste streams has the two-way screw, an inlet                and an outlet. The inlet and outlet are connected                to respective associated sections of the                multi-section pyrolysis system. The two-way screw                is adapted to convey the mixed waste stream through                the processing chamber, and to an associated                section of the multisection pyrolysis system when a                target selected from temperature, processing time,                texture, viscosity or porosity of the mixed waste                stream is reached. Several heating elements are                arranged in or on the chamber and adapted to heat                the mixed waste stream. A motor is adapted to                rotate at least the axle of the two-way screw                conveyor. The processing chamber being a de-gasser                adapted to flight and mix the mixed waste stream.                An additive canal is adapted to provide an additive                to the mixed waste stream within the processing                chamber and comprises a one-way valve or valve                system;    a pyrolysis system for upcycling mixed waste                streams has a densifier and a pyrolysis reactor.                The densifier and the pyrolysis reactor are                directly or indirectly fluidically connected. The                two-way screw is adapted as a conveyor and mixing                mechanism within at least one of the de-gasser and                the processing chamber of the multi-section                pyrolysis system;    a method of heating and mixing a stream of                mixed waste material in a pyrolysis system involves                feeding the mixed waste material at the inlet or                inlet end. The axle is rotated. The mixed waste                stream is conveyed to an associated section of the                pyrolysis system, when a target selected from                temperature, processing time, texture or porosity                of the mixed waste stream is reached; and    a method of manufacturing an upcycled material                from a mixed waste stream involve providing the                pyrolysis system and providing the mixed waste                stream. The mixed waste stream is processed through                the pyrolysis system. Oil is provided from the                mixed waste stream. At least a portion of the mixed                waste stream is processed into ash or black                carbon.          [retrieved on          20230531],relevantClaims[3],relevantPassages[&lt;pp&gt;4&lt;/pp&gt;]						IN202531076092-A	Producing composite material useful in photo            frames, clocks, toys, and stationery items, involves            melting expanded polystyrene at controlled temperature            and mechanically blending it with shredded optical lens            particles to form moldable pulp	GOEL P;  MONDAL S	UNIV KOLKATA BRAINWARE (UYKO-Non-standard)	202583648S	   NOVELTY - The method for producing a composite material                by upcycling expanded polystyrene and optical lens                waste, involves melting expanded polystyrene at a                controlled temperature and mechanically blending it                with shredded optical lens particles to form a                moldable pulp.    USE - Method for producing a composite material                useful to form photo frames, clocks, toys, and                stationery items (claimed).    ADVANTAGE - The composite material exhibits lightweight,                water resistance, and mechanical durability                suitable for consumer product applications. The                process requires no use of chemical solvents or                toxic reagents, thus ensuring environmental safety                and suitability for small-scale, decentralized                operations. The final molded products are coated                with a non-toxic, water-based or bio-based finish                to enhance surface smoothness and durability. The                system is operable by micro-enterprises, Self-Help                Groups (SHGs), and community-level entrepreneurs                using low-cost equipment. The device not only                addresses plastic waste management challenges but                also promotes circular economy practices and                livelihood generation. The method lies at the                intersection of environmental engineering, polymer                processing, and decentralized manufacturing,                offering an eco-friendly, scalable solution to                mitigate the harmful effects of plastic pollution                while enabling value-added product development. The                eco-products manufactured through this method have                significant commercial appeal due to their                sustainability, handcrafted aesthetics, and                customization potential. This creates opportunities                for green branding and storytelling under themes                like reborn from trash or eco-crafted. The                mechanical properties of the products have been                tested in initial lab trials and exhibit high                structural integrity, resilience to breakage, and                thermal stability. The process avoids the release                of hazardous emissions and produces no chemical                waste, making it suitable for safe indoor or                semi-industrial environments. Burn tests confirm                that the composite does not emit pungent odors or                toxic smoke under normal heating conditions,                validating the environmental safety of the process.                The modular design allows scalability, meaning the                same process can be adopted in larger facilities                using automated or semi-automated machinery for                higher throughput. By employing                moderate-temperature melting and mechanical                blending techniques, the invention successfully                overcomes the recycling challenges associated with                these heterogeneous plastic types. The resulting                composite material demonstrates excellent                workability, thermal stability, water resistance,                and mechanical strength, making it suitable for a                wide array of applications including home decor,                educational tools, and utility items.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for: (1) a                system for producing molded consumer products from                plastic waste, which has a shredding unit for                lenses, an expanded polystyrene melting chamber, a                mixing unit for blending, and a molding station for                casting the composite pulp into desired shape; and                (2) a composite material formed by the process of                blending shredded optical lens particles with                molten expanded polystyrene. (b) a separation module configured                to separate the terephthalate from the glycol; and                (c) a polymerization module configured to receive                terephthalate and glycol to form a third                composition comprising a second polyester and a                second alcohol, where first alcohol used in                depolymerization module comprises at least a                portion of second alcohol formed in polymerization                module.    glycol recovery system;    glycol waste recovery system;    producing recycled polyethylene terephthalate                (r-PET) using integrated process comprising (a)                subjecting a waste polyester feedstock comprising                PET to a depolymerization process, where the waste                polyester feedstock comprises 75-99 wt.% PET, and                the depolymerization comprises (i) mixing the waste                polyester feedstock with at least one portion of a                solvent, thus forming a first reaction mixture and                (ii) mixing the first reaction mixture with a                depolymerization catalyst, thus forming a second                reaction mixture comprising a solid fraction and a                liquid fraction, (b) performing a first                purification and the first purification comprises                melting the solid fraction to form a dimethyl                terephthalate (DMT) melt, and distilling the DMT                melt to yield r-DMT, (c) performing a second                purification, and the second purification comprises                (i) neutralizing the liquid fraction with an acid                to form a neutralized mixture, (ii) subjecting the                neutralized mixture to at least two distillations                to yield a methanol distillate and a monoethylene                glycol (MEG) distillate, the methanol distillate                comprises a recycled methanol (r-methanol) content                and the MEG distillate comprises a recycled MEG                (r-MEG) content and (d) subjecting the r-DMT                content and the r-MEG content to a polymerization                process, thus forming r-PET;    recovering EG comprising collecting a mixture                comprising EG from vapor streams of a                polymerization process, where the polymerization                process comprises an ester exchanger phase and a                melt phase polycondensation phase, evaporating the                mixture to form a liquid phase and a gaseous phase,                subjecting the liquid phase to at least one                separation process to form a waste fraction and a                collection fraction and subjecting the waste                fraction to a purification process comprising                neutralizing the waste fraction with at least one                portion of an acid to form a neutralized mixture,                adding a portion of methanol to the neutralized                mixture, and subjecting the neutralized mixture to                a first distillation to form a first distillate and                a first bottom, thus recovering methanol from the                first distillate and subjecting the first bottom to                subsequent distillations, thus recovering EG from a                final distillate;    producing a polyester, comprising a                depolymerization process and a polymerization                process, where the depolymerization process                comprises adding a first alcohol to a first                composition comprising a first polyester, and                subjecting the first polyester and the first                alcohol to conditions that the first polyester                depolymerizes to form a second composition                comprising an ester derivative and the                polymerization process comprises subjecting the                ester derivative to conditions that the ester                derivative polymerizes to form a third composition                comprising a second polyester and a second                alcohol;    eparating diol to the mono-alcohol process,                comprising providing a mixture comprising a                mono-alcohol, a diol, and an alkoxide, adding an                acid to the mixture and distilling the mixture to                produce a distillate comprising an increased ratio                of the mono-alcohol to the diol compared to the                mixture and (ii) a bottom comprising an increased                ratio of the diol to the mono-alcohol compared to                the mixture;  CHAUHAN M;  MANNA K	INDIAN INST TECHNOLOGY DELHI (IITD-C)	202535601Q	   NOVELTY - Isoreticular metal organic framework comprises                single-site metal-hydride, dihydride or alkyl                catalyst having varying pore sizes. The single-site                metal-hydride, dihydride or -alkyl catalyst ensures                a systematic cleavage of the carbon-carbon (sp3)                bond via hydrogenolysis of polymers into targeted                liquid products within a determined molecular                weight range.    USE - Isoreticular metal organic framework used for                shape selective hydrogenolysis of polyethylene                (claimed).    ADVANTAGE - The isoreticular metal organic framework can                produce a mixture of gaseous and liquid/wax                hydrocarbons with 100 % conversion and less than 7%                selectivity for gases, and enable highly efficient                upcycling of plastic wastes under mild conditions                owing to their unique combination of coordinatively                unsaturated single-site Ru-active sites, uniform                and tunable pores, well-defined porous structure,                and superior stability.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for: 1. a                process of synthesis of an isoreticular single-site                metal organic framework catalyst, which involves:                (a) synthesizing UiO-66 (Universitetet i Oslo 66),                using 1,4-benzenedicarboxylic acid and zirconium                chloride (ZrCl4) in dimethylformamide (DMF),                dissolving benzenedicarboxylic acid of about 0.05                to 0.25 g in about 1.2 to 6 mL DMF;(b)dissolving                ZrCl4of about 65-345mg in about 1.2 to 6 mL DMF to                form a solution;(c) mixing and heating solution at                about 100-130&#8451;; (d)centrifuging the                solution to obtain a white solid of UiO-66 in                tetrahydrofuran (THF);(d) synthesizing                UiO-66-ruthenium dichloride (RuCl2) by adding                n-Butyllithium (n-BuLi) to the slurry of UiO-66to                form a mixture;(e) stirring the mixture to form a                solid;(f)washing the solid with THF to remove the                excess n-BuLi;(g)adding a THF solution of                ruthenium(III) chloride trihydrate (RuCl3.3H2O) to                the washed solid to form a mixture; (h)stirring the                mixture at room temperature to obtain a suspension;                (i)washing the resultant black solid with THF and                ethyl acetate to remove any excess ruthenium to                obtain UiO-66-RuCl2; and 2. a process of shape                selective hydrogenolysis of polyethylene using                hydrogen (H2) in presence of metal organic                framework (MOF) catalyst, which involves: charging                MOF catalyst and polyethylene into a high pressure                reactor, sealing and charging the reactor with                hydrogen, pressurizing the reactor with H2and                heating at 150 to 250&#8451;, where the product                obtained is analyzed by gas chromatography                (GC). J04-B01C5;  J04-E01;  J04-E04B;  J04-E10;  J04-E11;  A04-G02A;  A09-B;  A10-E13	B01J-020/22;  B01J-027/20;  B01J-031/16;  B01J-031/22;  C01F-017/00;  C07B-035/02;  C08F-004/64	IN202311064737-A   28 Mar 2025   B01J-031/22   202537   English	IN202311064737-A    IN11064737    27 Sep 2023		IN11064737    27 Sep 2023					1013-0-0-0 					WO2024264042-A2;  WO2024264042-A3	New recombinant Pseudomonas putida group bacterium            useful for degrading polyethylene terephthalate,            comprises deleted or non-functional ped operon, and            recombinant nucleic acid molecules encoding large            subunit of terephthalate 1,2-dioxygenase and            terephthalate transporter	LU T;  BOA T;  QIAN Y;  XIN Y	UNIV ILLINOIS FOUND (UNII-C)	202500191F	   NOVELTY - A recombinant Pseudomonas putidagroup                bacterium (A), is new. The recombinant                P.putidagroup bacterium (A) comprises (a) a deleted                or non-functional ped operon and recombinant                nucleic acid molecules encoding a large subunit of                terephthalate 1,2-dioxygenase (tpaAa), small                subunit of terephthalate 1,2-dioxygenase (tpaAb),                ferredoxin reductase subunit (fpaB), dihydrodiol                dehydrogenase (tpaC), and terephthalate transporter                (tpaK), or (b) a deleted or non-functional                repressor gene of a glycolate dehydrogenase (Gel)                pathway (gclR), and a recombinant strong                constitutive promoter operably linked to a glcDEF                operon.    USE - The recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (A) is                useful in bacterial culture (A) for degrading PET.                The recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (B) is                useful in bacterial culture (B) for making medium                chain length polyhydroxyalkanoate (mcl-PHA                (6-14C)). The recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium                (C) is useful in bacterial culture (C) for making                muconate. The recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium                (D) is useful in bacterial culture (D) for                performing simultaneous mcl-PHA and itaconic acid                production (claimed).    ADVANTAGE - The recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (A)                ensures sustainable production of high valued                products from plastic waste, exhibits faster                deconstruction when substrate concentrations are                high or crude hydrolyzate is used, outperforms                monoculture for upcycling when polyhydroxyalkanoate                serves as a target product, produces desired                chemicals through division of labor, and degrades                99% or more TPA and EG present in the hydrolyzed                PET product.    DETAILED DESCRIPTION - INDEPENDENT CLAIMS are included for the                following: (1) a bacterial culture (A) comprising                the recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (A); (2) a                method (A) for degrading polyethylene terephthalate                (PET) involves mixing hydrolyzed PET product with a                growth medium, and adding the bacterial culture (A)                to a mixture of the hydrolyzed PET product and the                growth medium; (3) a recombinant P.putidagroup                bacterium (B) comprising (a) (i) a deleted or                non-functional ped operon, (ii) recombinant nucleic                acid molecules encoding tpaAa, tpaAb, fpaB, tpaC,                and tpaK, and (iii) recombinant nucleic acid                molecules encoding hydroxyacyl-ACP acyltransferase                (phaG), acyl-CoA-synthase (alkK), mcl-PHA synthase                1 (phaC1) and mcl-PHA synthase 2 (phaC2), or (b)                (i) a deleted or non-functional gclR gene, (ii) a                recombinant strong constitutive promoter operably                linked to a glcDEF operon, and (iii) recombinant                nucleic acid molecules encoding phaG, alkK, phaC1                and phaC2; (4) a bacterial culture (B) comprising                the recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (B); (5) a                method (B) for making medium chain length                polyhydroxyalkanoate (mcl-PHA (C6 to C14)) involves                mixing a hydrolyzed PET product with a growth                medium, and adding the bacterial culture (B) to a                mixture of the hydrolyzed PET product and the                growth medium, where mcl-PHA (6-14C) is produced;                (6) a recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (C)                comprising (a) (i) a deleted or non-functional ped                operon, (ii) recombinant nucleic acid molecules                encoding tpaAa, tpaAb, fpaB, tpaC, and tpaK, (iii)                a deleted or non-functional catRBCA cluster, (iv) a                recombinant nucleic acid molecule encoding PCA                decarboxylase (aroY), and (v) a recombinant nucleic                acid molecule encoding decarboxylase enhancer                (ecdB), or (b) (i) a deleted or non-functional gclR                gene, (ii) a recombinant strong constitutive                promoter operably linked to a glcDEF operon, (iii)                a deleted or non-functional catRBCA cluster, and                (iv) a recombinant strong constitutive promoter                operably linked to a nucleic acid molecule encoding                major catechol 1,2-dioxygenase (catA); (7) a                bacterial culture (C) comprising the recombinant                P.putidagroup bacterium (C); (8) a method (C) for                making muconate (MA) involves mixing a hydrolyzed                PET product with a growth medium, and adding the                recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (C) to a                mixture of the hydrolyzed PET product and the                growth medium, where MA is produced; (9) a                recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (D) comprising                (a) a deleted or non-functional ped operon and                recombinant nucleic acid molecules encoding tpaAa,                tpaAb, fpaB, tpaC and tpaK, and reduced expression                of icd, a recombinant nucleic acid molecule                encoding acn, recombinant nucleic acid molecules                encoding phaG, alkK, phaC1, and phaC2, which are                operably linked to one or more strong constitutive                promoters and a recombinant nucleic acid molecule                encoding cadA, or (b) a deleted or non-functional                repressor gene of the glycolate dehydrogenase (Glc)                pathway (glcR), and a recombinant strong                constitutive promoter operably linked to a glcDEF                operon, a recombinant ribosome binding site                operably linked to the glcDEF operon, reduced                expression of icd, a recombinant nucleic acid                molecule encoding acn, recombinant nucleic acid                molecules encoding phaG, alkK, phaC1 and phaC2                operably linked to one or more strong or                constitutive promoters, and a recombinant nucleic                acid molecule encoding cadA; (10) a bacterial                culture (D) comprising the recombinant                P.putidagroup bacterium (D); and (11) a method (D)                for performing simultaneous mcl-PHA and itaconic                acid production involves mixing a hydrolyzed PET                product with a growth medium, and adding the                recombinant P.putidagroup bacterium (D) to a                mixture of the hydrolyzed PET product and the                growth medium, where mcl-PHA and itaconic acid is                produced.
